y=\mathrm{Tr}{\rho_{0}\tilde{A}^{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mu_{i}(A_{ii}-x)^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}|A_{ji}|^{2}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}).

(81)

Also from (
78
) we obtain

\hat{H}:=\frac{1}{y}\begin{bmatrix}B_{1,1}&B_{2,1}^{*}&\dots&B_{r,1}^{*}&\vline&C^{*}_{r+1,1}&C^{*}_{r+2,1}&\dots&C^{*}_{d,1}\\
B_{2,1}&B_{2,2}&\ddots&\vdots&\vline&C^{*}_{r+1,2}&C^{*}_{r+2,2}&\ddots&\vdots\par\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&B_{r,r-1}^{*}&\vline&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&C^{*}_{d,r-1}\\
B_{r,1}&\dots&B_{r,r-1}&B_{r,r}&\vline&C^{*}_{r+1,r}&\dots&C^{*}_{d-1,r}&C^{*}_{d,r}\\
\hline\cr C_{r+1,1}&C_{r+1,2}&\dots&C_{r+1,r}&\vline&0&0&\dots&0\\
C_{r+2,1}&C_{r+2,2}&\ddots&\vdots&\vline&0&0&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&C_{d-1,r}&\vline&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&0\\
C_{d,1}&\dots&C_{d,r-1}&C_{d,r}&\vline&0&\dots&0&0\\
\end{bmatrix}

(82)

with the following relations

\displaystyle B_{i,i}

\displaystyle=(A_{ii}-x)\mu_{i}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq r

\displaystyle B_{j,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{ji}}{2}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j})\text{ for }i<j\leq r

\displaystyle C_{j,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{ji}}{2}\mu_{i}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq r,r+1\leq j\leq d.

Comparing equations (
80
) and (
82
) we get

u_{i}=t\frac{(A_{ii}-x)\mu_{i}}{y}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq r,\quad\zeta_{i,j}=t\frac{A_{ji}}{2y}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j})\text{ for }1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d

(83)

where
\mu_{j}=0
for
j>r
. Also let
\breve{u}_{i}=\frac{(A_{ii}-x)\mu_{i}}{y}
and
\breve{\zeta}_{i,j}=\frac{A_{ji}}{2y}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j})
.

Recall that after defining
\mathfrak{z}_{ij}=\zeta_{ij}/\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}
and replacing
\vartheta
by
\theta
, limiting model for
\rho^{\otimes n}_{\theta/\sqrt{n}}
is given by equation (17) and is given by

\displaystyle\Phi^{\theta,r}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(\tilde{u},V_{\mu})\otimes\bigotimes_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\phi_{\beta_{ij}}^{\mathfrak{z}_{ij}}\otimes\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r\\
r+1\leq j\leq d\end{subarray}}\phi_{\infty}^{\mathfrak{z}_{ij}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(t\breve{u},V_{\mu})\otimes\bigotimes_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\mathbb{N}_{2}\left(t\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}(Re\breve{\zeta}_{ij},Im\breve{\zeta}_{ij}),\frac{\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}{2(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}I_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\otimes\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r\\
r+1\leq j\leq d\end{subarray}}\mathbb{N}_{2}\left(t\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mu_{i}}}(Re\breve{\zeta}_{ij},Im\breve{\zeta}_{ij}),\frac{1}{2}I_{2}\right).

Note that an alternate expression for the limiting state is given by
\mathfrak{N}(t\tau,\mathscr{S})
where
\Sigma
is given as in (19) with
\sigma^{2}_{\beta_{ij}}=\frac{\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}{2(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}
for
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
and
V=V_{\mu}
. Also the shift is given by

\tau=\breve{u}\oplus{\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\left(Re\breve{\zeta}_{ij},Im\breve{\zeta}_{ij}\right).

Denoting
\hat{\Psi}=\Psi(\rho_{0})+n^{-1/2}\hat{t}
, we have the following chain of inequalities

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{\Psi}}\sup_{\rho\in\Sigma_{n,\varepsilon}(\rho_{0})}E_{\rho}|n^{1/2}(\hat{\Psi}-\Psi(\rho))|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\hat{t}}\sup_{|t|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}E_{\rho_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{|\hat{t}|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}\sup_{|t|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}E_{\mathfrak{N}_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}+o(1)

where
\mathfrak{N}_{t}:=\mathfrak{N}(t\tau,\mathscr{S})
. We observe that it is the estimator
\hat{t}
can also be restricted to a ball of radius
n^{\varepsilon}
without increasing the risk. Also for the same restriction of
t
and
\hat{t}_{n}
the supremum of the the loss
E_{\rho_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}
is
O(n^{2\varepsilon})
and multiplying by the equivalence rate of the reverse channel we have
n^{2\varepsilon-\kappa}=o(1)
(for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
) so that using (21) and (23) we obtain the second inequality.

Now construct the following prior
\pi
on
t
:
t\sim\mathcal{N}(0,n^{\varepsilon_{0}})
with
\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon
. The minimax risk can be lower bounded as follows:

\inf_{|\hat{t}|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}\sup_{|t|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}E_{\mathfrak{N}_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}\geq\inf_{|\hat{t}|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}\int_{|t|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}E_{\mathfrak{N}_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}d\pi(t).

Using an argument similar to the proof of Lemma
C.7
we can show that the RHS is further bounded by
\inf_{|\hat{t}|\leq n^{\varepsilon}}\int E_{\mathfrak{N}_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}d\pi(t)+o(1)
and since taking an infimum over a larger class of estimators (i.e. if
\hat{t}
is allowed to be unrestricted) decreases the risk, we obtain

\inf_{\hat{\Psi}}\sup_{\rho\in\Sigma_{n,\varepsilon}(\rho_{0})}E_{\rho}|n^{1/2}(\hat{\Psi}-\Psi(\rho))|^{2}\geq\inf_{\hat{t}}\int E_{\mathfrak{N}_{t}}|\hat{t}-t|^{2}d\pi(t)+o(1).

By (
53
) the first term in RHS is given by
\frac{\left(\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau\right)^{-1}n^{\epsilon_{0}}}{\left(\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau\right)^{-1}+n^{\epsilon_{0}}}
and hence we obtain

\liminf_{n}\inf_{\hat{\Psi}}\sup_{\rho\in\Sigma_{n,\varepsilon}(\rho_{0})}E_{\rho}|n^{1/2}(\hat{\Psi}-\Psi(\rho))|^{2}\geq(\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau)^{-1}.

Plugging in the value of
\Sigma
and
\tau
we obtain

\displaystyle\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau

\displaystyle=\breve{u}^{T}V^{-1}_{\mu}\breve{u}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\frac{4|\breve{\zeta}_{ij}|^{2}}{\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r\\
r+1\leq j\leq d\end{subarray}}\frac{4|\breve{\zeta}_{ij}|^{2}}{\mu_{i}}

\displaystyle=\breve{u}^{T}V^{-1}_{\mu}\breve{u}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}\frac{|A_{ji}|^{2}}{y^{2}}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}).

where the last line is obtained by plugging in the values of
\breve{\zeta}_{ij}
and letting
\mu_{j}=0
for
j>r
.

By example 3.1 of
[
56
]

\left[V^{-1}_{\mu}\right]_{ij}=\mu^{-1}_{i}\delta_{ij}+(1-\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\mu_{k})^{-1}
and hence noting that
1-\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\mu_{k}=\mu_{r}
we have

\displaystyle\breve{u}^{T}V^{-1}_{\mu}\breve{u}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r-1}\mu_{i}\mu_{j}(A_{ii}-x)(A_{jj}-x)(\mu^{-1}_{i}\delta_{ij}+\mu_{r}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\mu_{i}(A_{ii}-x)^{2}+\mu_{r}^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r-1}A_{ii}A_{jj}\mu_{i}\mu_{j}-\mu_{r}^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r-1}xA_{jj}\mu_{i}\mu_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\mu_{r}^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r-1}A_{ii}x\mu_{i}\mu_{j}+\mu_{r}^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r-1}x^{2}\mu_{i}\mu_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\mu_{i}(A_{ii}-x)^{2}+\mu_{r}^{-1}(x-A_{rr}\mu_{r})^{2}-2x\mu_{r}^{-1}(1-\mu_{r})(x-A_{rr}\mu_{r})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\mu_{r}^{-1}x^{2}(1-\mu_{r})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{2}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\mu_{i}(A_{ii}-x)^{2}+\mu_{r}(A_{rr}-x)^{2}\right]

Thus we obtain

\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau=\frac{1}{y^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mu_{i}(A_{ii}-x)^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}\frac{|A_{ji}|^{2}}{y^{2}}(\mu_{i}+\mu_{j})

Plugging back the expression of
y
from equation (
81
) we observe that the RHS of the above equation is
y^{-1}
and hence we have

\liminf_{n}\inf_{\hat{\Psi}}\sup_{\rho\in\Sigma_{n,\varepsilon}(\rho_{0})}E_{\rho}|n^{1/2}(\hat{\Psi}-\Psi(\rho))|^{2}\geq y=V^{2}_{\rho_{0}}.

∎

Remark
: Note that if we had a multivariate Gaussian
\mathcal{N}_{k}(t\tau,\Sigma)
with
k=r-1+r(r-1)/2+r(d-r)
, then it can be easily shown that the minimax risk for estimation of
t
is
(\tau^{T}\Sigma^{-1}\tau)^{-1}
. So the minimax risk for the classical model and the classical-quantum model matches for this particular case where the shift is indexed by
1
-dimensional parameter.

Appendix D
Proof of technical lemmas

D.1
Auxiliary lemma for channel construction

First we set some notations and prove a technical lemma (the equivalent of Lemma 7.1 of
[
36
]
). We note that the some statements of the lemma differs because of the low-rank structure. Although one can prove the statements by suitably modifying Lemma 7.1 of
[
36
]
we include the proof of the entire lemma (and some other lemmas in the following subsection)
mutatis mutandis
for the convenience of the reader.
However the explanations will be brief and the reader can refer to the discussion preceding Lemma 7.1 of
[
36
]
for further clarification.

1) Let
l(c)
be the length of the column
c
in the Young diagram
\lambda
. Note that there are
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i+1}
columns such that
l(c)=i
. Also, in our case
l(c)\leq r
since the Young diagrams can only have
r
-rows in our setup.

2) Recall that
f_{\mathbf{a}}=f_{\mathbf{a}(1)}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{\mathbf{a}(n)}
are the basis vectors and we associate a Young tableau
t_{\bf a}
with each
f_{\mathbf{a}}
where the indices
\bf{a}(i)
fill the boxes of a diagram
\lambda
row-wise.
Let
t^{c}_{a}
be the content of the column
c
of
t_{\bf a}
, i.e. the function

t_{\bf a}^{c}:\{1,\dots,l(c)\}\rightarrow\{1,\dots,d\}

which gives the column entries of the column
c
. For example, if

t_{\bf a}
is given by the following semi-standard Young tableau

1

3

2

3

we get the values:

\displaystyle t_{\bf a}^{1}(1)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle t_{\bf a}^{1}(2)

\displaystyle=2,

\displaystyle t_{\bf a}^{2}(1)

\displaystyle=2,

\displaystyle t_{\bf a}^{2}(2)

\displaystyle=3,

\displaystyle t_{\bf a}^{3}(1)

\displaystyle=3.

3) We also use the alternate notation
f_{\bf m}
for
f_{\bf a}
(with
{\bf m}=\{m_{i,j}:1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d\}
, where
m_{i,j}
is the number of
j
’s in the row
i
of
t_{\bf a}
) and that
y_{\lambda}f_{\bf m}=q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}f_{\bf m}
spans
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}
where
t_{\bf{a}}
is a semi-standard tableau. Let
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
be the orbit of
f_{\bf m}
under the
subgroup
\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}
of row permutations i.e. the set of vectors

f_{\mathbf{b}}
which have exactly
m_{i,j}
boxes with
j
in row
i
, and the rest are
i
. Note that a semi-standard tableau ensures that if we permute among rows only, row
i
has no entries smaller than
i
. As a consequence of the orbit-stabilizer theorem we have

p_{\lambda}f_{\bf m}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}}f_{{\bf a}\circ\sigma}=\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}\frac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}}{\#\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}{f_{\bf b}}.

(84)

4) If a column contains two identical entries it does not contribute to
y_{\lambda}f_{\bf a}
since we are antisymmetrizing with
q_{\lambda}
. Thus only tableaux

t_{\bf a}
(not necessarily semistandard after the action of
p_{\lambda}
) which do not have two equal entries in the same column are important and we call them admissible ones and denote the collection of associated vectors
f_{\bf a}
by
\mathcal{V}
.

5) For any
f_{\bf a}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
we define

\Gamma(f_{\mathbf{a}}):=|{\bf m}|-\#\{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}:{t_{\bf a}^{c}}\neq{\rm Id}^{c}\},

and denote by
\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m})
the set of vectors

f_{\bf a}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})\bigcap\mathcal{V}
with

\Gamma(f_{a})=\Gamma
. Then we have

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})\bigcap\mathcal{V}=\bigcup_{\Gamma\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m}).

It can be easily seen that
\Gamma(f_{\bf a})\geq 0
and is zero if and only if each column

{t_{\bf a}^{c}}
is either
{\rm Id}^{c}
or of the form
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(r)=j\delta_{r=i}+r\delta_{r\neq i}
for some
i\leq l(c)<j
. A tableau
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}
of the latter form will be called an
(i,j)
-substitution
.

One then considers the following ‘algorithm’ to build all the possible
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m})
.

Algorithm

For a given
({\bf m},\lambda)
we generate a particular admissible
f_{\bf a}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
by selecting the
m_{i,j}
boxes on row
i
which are filled with
j
, for all
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
. To ensure admissibility we have to ensure that no column should have two boxes filled with the same number. Start with
f_{\bf 0}:=f_{{\bf m}={\bf 0}}
(row
i
is filled with

i
’s only), and with a set of
|{\bf m}|
“bricks" containing

m_{i,j}
identical bricks labelled
(i,j)
, for
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
. These bricks are placed sequentially i.e. for a particular
(i,j)
brick we replace the content of a box from row
i
with
j
(initially the content was
i
). Once the bricks have been placed the content of each column c (i.e
t_{\bf a}^{c}
) is uniquely defined by the set of bricks
\kappa
which shall be called a
column-modifier
.

For example if
\kappa=\{(i,j),(f,l)\}
then the column has entries

t_{\bf a}^{c}(k)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}j&\textrm{if $k=i$};\\
l&\textrm{if $k=f$};\\
k&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

A column modifier of the form
\kappa(i,j)
is called elementary and can only be used in a column with
i\leq l(c)<j
(otherwise the entry
j
would appear twice making the column inadmissible). Now, since the length of a column is at most
r
and all entries must be different, there are less than
d!
(although the upper bound is
d!/(d-r)!
we use the
d!
since a sharper bound does not change the asymptotics) different types of column-modifiers. Note that a column-modifier always increases the value of the modified cells, so that in this case
t^{c}_{a}(\{1,\dots,l(c)\})\neq\{1,\dots,l(c)\}
.

To count the total number of ways the tableau can be formed we can count the total number of ways the columns are modified. A given collection of column-modifiers is uniquely determined by
\{m_{\kappa},\kappa\}
where
m_{\kappa}
is the multiplicity of
\kappa
. We consider the following procedure:

I.

Choose
\Gamma
bricks among our
|{\bf m}|
. As we have at most
rd

different types of bricks there are at most
(rd)^{\Gamma}
possibilities.

II.

Recall that
|\textbf{m}|-\Gamma
is the total number of modified columns. As this is the exact number of bricks left after the first stage we can consider them as a set of elementary column-modifiers. Indeed if all the column modifiers were elementary, then
\Gamma=0
. If
\Gamma\neq 0
, we form non-elementary column modifiers from elementary ones by sequentially adding each of the
\Gamma
bricks selected in the first stage, to some of the elementary column-modifiers.
At each step there are at most
d!
different choices (of column modifiers) to which we can attach the new brick. Hence, we have at most
(d!)^{\Gamma}
possibilities. At the end of stage II at least
\max\{0,|{\bf m}|-2\Gamma\}
of the column-modifiers are elementary, and we note that
m_{\kappa(i,j)}\leq m_{i,j}
.

III.

We apply the column-modifiers to the columns of
f_{\mathbf{0}}
, with the constraints that no two modifiers are applied to the same column and the resulting

f_{\bf{a}}
is admissible.

While inserting the column modifiers we note that for a non-elementary column modifier
\kappa
there are less than n possibilities. In the case of an elementary one of type
\kappa(i,j)
we have the constraints that it can only be inserted in a column with at least
i
rows, and since the column cannot contain another
j
(to ensure admissibility) its length has to be smaller than
j
. Noting that number of such columns is
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}
, we give an upper bound to the number of possibilities at stage three of the algorithm as follows:

\displaystyle\prod_{\kappa\neq\kappa({i,j})}\frac{n^{m_{\kappa}}}{m_{\kappa}!}\cdot\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{\kappa(i,j)}}}{m_{\kappa(i,j)}!}

(85)

the combinatorial factor
\prod_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}!
(
m_{\kappa}
being the number of column modifiers of type
\kappa
) appearing to account for the identical columns. If
\Gamma=0
, then all column modifiers are elementary and for each
\kappa(i,j)
,
the number of available columns is at least

(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-\lvert{\bf m}|)_{+}:=0\wedge\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-\lvert{\bf m}|
.
Thus we have the following lower bound

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-|\mathbf{m}|)_{+}^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}.

(86)

6) We collect all multiplicities in

E:=\{m_{\kappa}^{\bf a}:\kappa\},

where
m_{\kappa}^{\bf a}
is the number of columns with
column-modifer
\kappa
, noting that
\Gamma
is a function of
E
and the following relation holds

\Gamma(f_{\bf a})=|{\bf m}|-\sum_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}^{\bf a}.

We note that the condition
\Gamma(f_{\bf a})=0
identifies the multiplicity
set
E^{0}
. In particular, we have
m_{\kappa(i,j)}=m_{i,j}
for all
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
and the other

m_{\kappa}=0
.
In the general case, we decompose the set
\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m})
in terms of
\mathcal{V}^{E}({\bf m})
(the set of tableaux in

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})\bigcap\mathcal{V}
with

E(f_{\bf a})=E
) as follows:

\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m})=\bigcup_{E}\mathcal{V}^{E}({\bf m}).

(87)

7) Finally to each column
c
of
t_{\bf a}
we associate the set of modified entries: each element of this set is of the form
(i,+)
or
(i,-)
depending on whether
i
is added or deleted. It is easy to verify that if
t_{\bf a}
is admissible, this modified set determines column-modifier
\kappa
associated to
c
. and hence shall be denoted by
S(\kappa)
. For example
S(\kappa(i,j))=\{(i,-),(j,+)\}
and for
\kappa=\{(i,j),(j,k)\}
we have

S(\kappa)=\{(i,-),(k,+)\}
.
We define the multiplicities
m_{S}^{\bf a}=\sum_{\kappa:S(\kappa)=S}m_{\kappa}^{\bf a}
and consider the set
F(f_{\bf a}):=\{m_{S}^{\bf a}:S\}
.

We are now ready to state the auxiliary lemma which will be crucial in proving Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
.

Lemma D.1
.

1.

For any unitary operator
U\in M(\mathbb{C}^{d})
,
for any basis vectors
{f_{\bf a}}
and
{f_{\bf b}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\det(U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{t_{\bf b}^{c}}}),

(88)

where
U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{t_{\bf b}^{c}}}
is the
{l(c)}\times{l(c)}
minor of
U
given by
[U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{t_{\bf b}^{c}}}]_{i,j}=U_{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(i),{t_{\bf b}^{c}}(j)}
.

Under the assumptions

\displaystyle|\mathbf{m}|

\displaystyle\leq n^{\eta},

(89)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\in\Lambda_{2},

\displaystyle\inf_{i}|\mu_{i}-\mu_{i+1}|

\displaystyle\geq\delta,

\displaystyle\mu_{r}

\displaystyle\geq\delta,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{z}\|

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{\beta},\qquad\beta\leq 1/2.

we have the following estimates with remainder terms uniform in the eigenvalues
\{\mu_{i}\}
:

2.

The number of admissible
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})

with
\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})=0
is

\#\mathcal{V}^{0}({\bf m})=\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}(1+O(n^{-1+2\eta}/\delta)).

(90)

3.

Let
E:=\{m_{\kappa}:\kappa\}
with
\Gamma(E)=\Gamma
.
The number of admissible
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
with
E({f_{\bf a}})=E
is bounded by:

\#\mathcal{V}^{E}({\bf m})\leq n^{-\Gamma+\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(m_{i,j}-m_{\kappa({i,j})})}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{\kappa({i,j})}}}{m_{\kappa({i,j})}!}.

(91)

4.

The number of admissible
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
with

\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})=\Gamma
is bounded by:

\#\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}({\bf m})\leq C^{\Gamma}n^{-\Gamma}\delta^{-2\Gamma}|\mathbf{m}|^{2\Gamma}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!},

(92)

for a constant
C=C(d)
.

5.

Let
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma^{a}}(\mathbf{l})
, and consider
\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma^{b}}({\bf m})\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})
for some fixed

\Gamma^{b}
. Then:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma^{b}}({\bf m})}{f_{\bf b}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mbox{if }\Gamma^{b}\neq|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|+\Gamma^{a}\\
(C|\mathbf{m}|)^{\Gamma^{b}}&\mbox{otherwise}\\
\end{array}\right.,

with
C=C(d)
.

6.

If
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}(\mathbf{m})
, then

\displaystyle\left\langle{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf b}}\right\rangle=1.

(95)

7.

If
f_{\bf a}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}({\bf m})
so that its set of elementary column-modifiers is

E^{0}=\{m_{\kappa(i,j)}=m_{i,j}\}
, then

\displaystyle\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}}{\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}r(n),

(96)

where

\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}_{2}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}|\mathfrak{z}|^{2}_{ij}

and the error factor is given by

r(n)=1+O\left(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1/2+2\beta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\beta+\alpha}\delta^{-1}\right).

8.

If
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{E}({\bf m})
, so that its set of column-modifiers is

E=\{m_{\kappa}:\kappa\}
and
\Gamma(E)=\Gamma
, then

\left|\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle\right|\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\left(\frac{C\|\mathfrak{z}\|}{\sqrt{n\delta}}\right)^{\Upsilon}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}}{\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\right)^{m_{\kappa({i,j})}}r(n)

(97)

where

\Upsilon=-\Gamma+\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(m_{i,j}-m_{\kappa({i,j})}),

C=C(d)
a constant and
r(n)
as in point
7
above.

9.

Under the further hypotheses that
\|\mathbf{z}\|\leq n^{\beta}
,
m_{i,j}\leq 2|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j}|n^{\beta+\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
,
we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U^{r}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n}){f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{\big((\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j})(\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}})\big)^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}r(n),

(98)

with

r(n)=1+O\left(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\beta+\alpha}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+\alpha+\eta}\delta^{-1},\delta^{-3/2}n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon}\right).

10.

Under the further hypotheses that
|\mathbf{l}|\leq|\mathbf{m}|
and
n^{1-3\eta}>2C/\delta^{2}
, where
C=C(d)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf b}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C|\mathbf{m}|)^{|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{l_{i,j}}}{l_{i,j}!}\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{l}|^{2}|\mathbf{m}|}{n\delta^{2}}\right)^{\Gamma^{a}_{\min}(\mathbf{l},\mathbf{m})}

(99)

with

\displaystyle\Gamma^{a}_{\min}(\mathbf{l},\mathbf{m})\geq\frac{\big(|\mathbf{l}-\mathbf{m}|+3|\mathbf{l}|-3|\mathbf{m}|\big)_{+}}{6}.

(100)

11.

We have

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf b}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}\big(1+O(n^{3\eta-1}/\delta)\big).

(101)

Proof.

Proof of (
88
).
Writing
f_{\mathbf{a}}=f_{\mathbf{a}(1)}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{\mathbf{a}(n)}
(and similarly for
{f_{\bf b}}
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\langle{f_{\bf a}}|U^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\prod_{1\leq r\leq{l(c)}}\langle f_{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(r)}|Uf_{{t_{\bf b}^{c}}(r)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\prod_{1\leq r\leq{l(c)}}U_{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(r),{t_{\bf b}^{c}}(r)}.

Since the subgroup of column permutations
\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}
is the product of the permutation groups of each column, each
\sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}
can be decomposed as

\sigma=s_{1}\dots s_{\lambda_{1}}

with
{s_{c}}
a permutation of column
c
which transforms
{t_{\bf b}^{c}}(r)
into
{t_{\bf b}^{c}}({s_{c}}(r))
. Then we have

\displaystyle\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle=\langle{f_{\bf a}}|U^{\otimes n}q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}}\epsilon(\sigma)\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\prod_{1\leq r\leq{l(c)}}U_{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(r),{t_{\bf b}^{c}}({s_{c}}(r))}

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\sum_{{s_{c}}\in S_{c}}\epsilon({s_{c}})\prod_{1\leq r\leq{l(c)}}U_{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(r),{t_{\bf b}^{c}}({s_{c}}(r))}

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}\det(U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{t_{\bf b}^{c}}}).

Proof of (
90
).

To find the number of admissible
{f_{\bf a}}
we multiply the possibilities of the three stages of the algorithm. For
\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})=0
the first two stages yield
1
as the combinatorial factor. Hence
\#\mathcal{V}^{0}
is the number of possibilities at the third stage of the algorithm. Thus from (
85
) we obtain the following upper bound for
\#\mathcal{V}^{0}({\bf m})
:
\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{i,j}}/m_{i,j}!
. Also, we note that from the conditions (
89
)
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}\geq\delta n/2
and

|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta}
, so that using (
86
) as a lower bound we obtain (
90
).

Proof of (
91
).

Since
E
is fixed, the number of
{f_{\bf a}}
in
\mathcal{V}^{E}
is given by the third stage of the algorithm. We then apply (
85
) with
\sum_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}=|\mathbf{m}|-\Gamma
. Finally we neglect the
m_{\kappa}!
factors to obtain (
91
).

Proof of (
92
).

Recalling (
87
), we note that it is sufficient to give an upper bound to
\#\mathcal{V}^{E}
since the first two stages of the algorithm yield a combinatorial factor of at most
C^{\Gamma}
, with
C=C(d)
.
Since
\sum m_{\kappa({i,j})}\geq|\mathbf{m}|-2\Gamma
, we have

\prod_{\kappa}m_{\kappa(i,j)}!\geq\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}m_{i,j}!M^{-2\Gamma}

where
M=\sup_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}m_{i,j}\leq|\mathbf{m}|
. Note that
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}\geq\delta n/2
and using equation ((
91
)) in conjunction with the above result we obtain

\#\mathcal{V}^{E}\leq n^{-\Gamma}\delta^{-2\Gamma}|\mathbf{m}|^{2\Gamma}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!},\qquad\forall E\penalty\ {\rm with}\penalty\ \Gamma(E)=\Gamma.

Finally we multiply by the number of possible
E
(i.e.
C^{\Gamma}
) to obtain the desired result.

Proof of (
5
).

Denote by
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}([1,l(c)])

the set of entries
\{{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(1),\dots,{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(l(c))\}
and consider equation (
88
) with
U=\mathbf{1}
.
Since both
{f_{\bf a}}
and
{f_{\bf b}}
are tensor product of basis vectors, the scalar product
\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle
is equal to
-1
or
1
if
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}([1,l(c)])={t_{\bf b}^{c}}([1,l(c)])
for all columns, and
0
otherwise. Since a modified column cannot satisfy
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}([1,l(c)])=[1,l(c)]

(and the same for
{\bf b}
) the condition given in the last line implies
\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle=0
unless
t_{\bf{a}}
and
t_{\bf{b}}
have the same number of modified columns. Since the total number of unmodified columns for
{f_{\bf a}}
and
{f_{\bf b}}
are
|\mathbf{l}|-\Gamma^{a}
and
|\mathbf{m}|-\Gamma^{b}
respectively, we obtain the first line of (
5
).

Now consider the case when
\Gamma^{b}=|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|+\Gamma^{a}
. Since

|\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle|\leq 1
, we can bound the LHS of (
5
) by the number of non-zero inner products
\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle
. In other words we want to count the number of
t_{\bf{b}}
such that
t_{\bf a}^{c}([1,l(c)])=t_{\bf b}^{c}([1,l(c)])
, or equivalently
S(\kappa_{\bf a}^{c})=S(\kappa_{\bf b}^{c})
. For building the relevant
{f_{\bf b}}
, we can follow the algorithm with the further condition that, at stage three the last condition (i.e.
S(\kappa_{\bf a}^{c})=S(\kappa_{\bf b}^{c})
) is met.

The first two stages of the algorithm yield a
C^{\Gamma^{b}}
factor. In the third stage,
for each
S
, we identify the column modifiers

\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{r(S)}
such that
S(\kappa_{i})=S
for all

1\leq i\leq r(S)
. The total number of such objects is
m_{S}:=\sum_{i\leq r(S)}m_{\kappa_{i}}
and the total number of ways in which they can be inserted to produce distinct tableaux is

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}m_{S}\\
m_{\kappa_{1}}\dots m_{\kappa_{r(S)}}\end{array}\right).

Recall that the total number of elementary column-modifiers
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}m_{\kappa(i,j)}
can be lower bounded by
|{\bf m}|-2\Gamma^{b}
and that each elementary column-modifier

\kappa(i,j)
corresponds to a different
S(\kappa(i,j))=\{(i,-),(j,+)\}
. Thus

|{\bf m}|-2\Gamma^{b}\leq\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}m_{\kappa(i,j)}\leq\sum_{S}\max_{\kappa:S(\kappa)=S}m_{\kappa}.

Since

\sum_{S}m_{S}=\sum_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}=|{\bf m}|-\Gamma^{b},

we obtain

\sum_{S}\left(m_{S}-\max_{\kappa:S(\kappa)=S}m_{\kappa}\right)\leq\Gamma^{b}.

This implies

\prod_{S}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}m_{S}\\
m_{\kappa_{1}}\dots m_{\kappa_{r(S)}}\end{array}\right)\leq|\mathbf{m}|^{\Gamma^{b}}.

Again we multiply by the
C^{\Gamma^{b}}
of the first two stages and get (
5
).

Proof of (
95
).

From (
5
) the only non-zero contributions come from

f_{\bf b}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
.
Since
\Gamma^{b}=0
, the combinatorial factor from the two first stages of the algorithm is
1
. Also since all the column modifiers are elementary we have
m_{S}=m_{i,j}=m_{\kappa({i,j})}
for all
S
(i.e.
0
for the non-elementary ones) and the combinatorial factor is again one. In other words, the only
{f_{\bf b}}
such that
\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle\neq 0
is
{f_{\bf a}}
. Finally it is easy to check that
\langle{f_{\bf a}}\mid q_{\lambda}{f_{\bf a}}\rangle=1
.

Proof of (
96
).

From (
88
) we have

\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle=\prod_{1\leq c\leq\lambda_{1}}{\rm det}(U^{t_{\bf a}^{c},{\rm Id}^{c}}),\qquad U=U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n}).

Now consider the following Taylor expansions. Entry-wise, for all
1\leq i\leq r
on the first line, and all
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
on the second and third lines:

\displaystyle U^{r}_{i,i}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i<j\leq d}\frac{|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|^{2}}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}-\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{1\leq j<i}\frac{|\mathfrak{z}_{j,i}|^{2}}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}+O(\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{3}n^{-3/2}\delta^{-3/2});

\displaystyle U^{r}_{i,j}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}^{*}}{\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}+O(\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}n^{-1}\delta^{-1});

\displaystyle U^{r}_{j,i}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}}{\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}+O(\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}n^{-1}\delta^{-1}).

If
\mathfrak{z}=O(n^{\beta})
and

\beta<1/2
,
the remainder terms are
O(n^{-3/2+3\beta}\delta^{-3/2})
for the first line. Similarly the remainder terms for the last two lines are

O(n^{-1+2\beta}\delta^{-1})
.
We use the Taylor expansions given above to find the dominating terms in the expansion
\det A=\sum_{\sigma}\prod_{i}\epsilon(\sigma)A_{i,\sigma(i)}.

Since
{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}
, all
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}
are either
{\rm Id}^{c}
, or an

(i,j)
-substitution
. If
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}={\rm Id}^{c}
,
the summands with more than two non-diagonal terms are of the same order as the remainder term, so we need to take into account only the identity and the transpositions in the sum
\sum_{\sigma}\prod_{i}A_{i,\sigma(i)}
. Let
l=l(c)
, then

\upsilon(l):=\det(U^{{\rm Id}^{c},{\rm Id}^{c}}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n}))=1-\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq l\\
l+1\leq j\leq d\end{subarray}}\frac{|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|^{2}}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}+O(n^{-3/2+3\beta}\delta^{-3/2}).

Consider now the case
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}\neq{\rm Id}^{c}
. Since
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(k)\geq k
for all
k
, there
exists a whole column of
U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{\rm Id}^{c}}
whose entries are smaller in
modulus than
O(\|\mathfrak{z}\|/\sqrt{n\delta})=O(n^{-1/2+\beta}\delta^{-1})
.
In particular if
{t_{\bf a}^{c}}
is an

(i,j)
-substitution
, then the only summand that is of this
order comes from the identity permutation. So that

\upsilon(i,j):=\det(U^{{t_{\bf a}^{c}},{\rm Id}^{c}}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n}))=\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}}{\sqrt{n(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}}+O(n^{-1+2\beta}\delta^{-1}).

(102)

Note that each
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
there are
m_{i,j}
columns of the type
(i,j)
-substitution. Out of the
\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}
columns of length
l=l(c)
, there are

\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}-R_{l}
of the type
{\rm Id}^{c}
, with
0\leq R_{l}\leq\lvert{\bf m}\rvert
. We are now ready to expand (
88
) in terms of our estimates.

\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle=\prod_{l=1}^{r}\left(\upsilon(l))\right)^{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\upsilon(i,j)\right)^{m_{i,j}}\prod_{l=1}^{r}(\upsilon(l))^{-R_{l}}.

(103)

Now
\upsilon(l)=1+O(n^{-1+2\beta}\delta^{-1})

and
R_{l}\leq|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta}
, so the last product is

1+O(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1})
.
Similarly, since
\lambda\in\Lambda_{2}
we have
\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}=n(\mu_{l}-\mu_{l+1})+O(n^{\alpha})
. We need the following lemma (see
[
36
]
) for an exponential approximation:

Lemma D.2
.

If
x_{n}=O(n^{1/2-\epsilon})
, then

\left(1+\frac{x_{n}}{n}\right)^{n}=\exp(x_{n})(1+O(n^{-\epsilon})).

Using
Lemma
D.2
we estimate the first product in (
103
) as follows

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{r}\upsilon(l)^{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1}^{r}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq l\\
l+1\leq j\leq d\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|^{2}\frac{\mu_{l}-\mu_{l+1}}{(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}\right)r(n)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}\sum_{l=i}^{r\wedge(j-1)}|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|^{2}\frac{\mu_{l}-\mu_{l+1}}{(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}\right)r(n)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)r(n),

(104)

with

\displaystyle r(n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+O(n^{-1+\alpha+2\beta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1/2+2\beta}\delta^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{z}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|^{2}.

Here we have used the relation that

\sum_{l=i}^{r\wedge(j-1)}\frac{\mu_{l}-\mu_{l+1}}{(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}=1

which is trivial for
j\leq r
and for
j>r
we have used the fact that both
\mu_{j}
and
\mu_{r+1}
are
0
.
We now estimate the middle product in the RHS of (
103
) as

\upsilon(i,j)^{m_{i,j}}=\left(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}}{\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\left(1+O\left(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1}\right)\right),

(105)

where we have used that
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}
. From (
103
), (
104
) and (
105
), we obtain (
96
).

Proof of (
97
).

We have the following analog of (
103
),

\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle=\prod_{l=1}^{r}\left(\upsilon(l))\right)^{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}}\prod_{\kappa}\left(\upsilon(\kappa)\right)^{m_{\kappa}}\prod_{l=1}^{r}(\upsilon(l))^{-R_{l}}

where
0\leq R_{l}\leq|{\bf m}|-\Gamma
and
\upsilon(\kappa)
is the determinant of the minor of
U
corresponding to the column where
\kappa
is applied. We further split the column-modifiers into elementary (
\kappa=\kappa(i,j)
) and non-elementary (say
\kappa=\kappa^{\prime})
ones. Then
\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle
can be written as

\prod_{l=1}^{r}\left(\upsilon(l))\right)^{\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{l+1}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\upsilon(i,j)\right)^{m_{\kappa(i,j)}}\prod_{l=1}^{r}(\upsilon(l))^{-R_{l}}\prod_{\kappa^{\prime}}\left(\upsilon(\kappa^{\prime})\right)^{m_{\kappa^{\prime}}}.

The first three products on the RHS can be approximated like the terms in (
103
).
To give an upper bound to the fourth product, we note that if the entries in the column have been modified in an admissible way, then

{t_{\bf a}^{c}}(i)=j>l(c)
for some
i
, so that
|\upsilon(\kappa)|\leq C\|\mathfrak{z}\|/\sqrt{n\delta}
for any
\kappa
, with some constant
C=C(d)
.

Thus we have

\left|\langle{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle\right|\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)\left(\frac{C\|\mathfrak{z}\|}{\sqrt{n\delta}}\right)^{\sum_{\kappa^{\prime}}m_{\kappa^{\prime}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r\\
i<j\leq d\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}|}{\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\right)^{m_{\kappa(i,j)}}r(n).

(106)

We obtain (
97
) by noting that the number of non-elementary modifiers is

\Upsilon=\sum_{\kappa^{\prime}}m_{\kappa^{\prime}}=-\Gamma+\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(m_{i,j}-m_{\kappa(i,j)}).

Proof of (
9
).

Recall that we identify
\mathbf{z}
with the vector
(\mathbf{z},0)
. We shall split the sum over
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
into sub-sums using the decomposition

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})\bigcap\mathcal{V}=\bigcup_{E}\mathcal{V}^{E}({\bf m})
, and compare each sub-sum against
\mathcal{V}^{0}=\mathcal{V}^{E^{0}}
. Then we aggregate all the
E
corresponding to a particular
\Gamma
(which results in a multiplication by a combinatorial factor) and finally sum over different
\Gamma
. From the given bounds on
\mathfrak{z}
and
\mathbf{z}
we note that

\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|=O(n^{\beta})
, so we can apply the previous results with

\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}
instead of
\mathfrak{z}
.

Using (
90
) and (
96
) and recalling that
\lambda\in\Lambda_{2}
, we get:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U^{r}(\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}/\sqrt{n})^{\otimes n}{f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{\big((\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j})\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}\big)^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}r(n)

with error factor

r(n)=1+O\left(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1/2+2\beta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\beta+\alpha}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+\alpha+\eta}\delta^{-1}\right).

For
E\neq E^{0}
we combine (
97
) and (
91
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{E}}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n}){f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle\right|\cdot\left|\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n}){f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle\right|^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{-\Gamma}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}{n}\right)^{m_{\kappa({i,j})}-m_{i,j}}\frac{m_{i,j}!}{m_{\kappa({i,j})}!}\left(\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|}{\sqrt{\delta n}}\right)^{-\Gamma}

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta n}|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j}|}{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}\right)^{m_{\kappa({i,j})}-m_{i,j}}r(n)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(n^{-\Gamma(1/2-\beta)})\delta^{-\Gamma/2}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d:m_{i,j}\neq 0}\left(\frac{|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j}|\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}}{m_{i,j}}\right)^{m_{\kappa(i,j)}-m_{i,j}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\big((2\delta^{-3/2}n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon})^{\Gamma}\big),

with
O(\cdot)
uniform in
\Gamma
. In the second inequality we have used

m_{i,j}!/m_{\kappa(i,j)}!\leq m_{i,j}^{m_{i,j}-m_{\kappa(i,j)}},\qquad\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(m_{\kappa({i,j})}-m_{i,j})\geq-2\Gamma,\qquad\lambda\in\Lambda_{2}

and in the third inequality we have used

m_{i,j}\leq 2|\mathfrak{z}_{i,j}+z_{i,j}|n^{\beta+\epsilon}.

Again note that for a given
\Gamma
, there are at most
C^{\Gamma}
different
E
such that
\Gamma(E)=\Gamma
(where
C=C(d)
) corresponding to the possible choices in the first two stages of the algorithm, . Hence, if
n
is large enough such that

2C\delta^{-3/2}n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon}<1
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n}){f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle=\sum_{\Gamma}\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma}}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}U((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n}){f_{\bf 0}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big(1+O(\delta^{-3/2}n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon})\Big)\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{\big((\mathfrak{z}+z)_{i,j}(\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}})\big)^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}r(n)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{\big((\mathfrak{z}+z)_{i,j}(\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}})\big)^{m_{i,j}}}{m_{i,j}!}r_{2}(n)

where the sum over
\Gamma
was bounded using a geometric series and

r_{2}(n)=1+O\left(n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+\alpha+\beta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+2\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+\alpha+\eta}\delta^{-1},\delta^{-3/2}n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon}\right).

Thus we obtain (
9
).

Proof of (
99
).

We choose
\Gamma^{a}
and
\Gamma^{b}
satisfying the condition

\Gamma^{b}-\Gamma^{a}=|{\bf m}|-|{\bf l}|
to ensure that the inner products in
(
5
) are non-zero. By multiplying (
92
) and (
5
), we see that:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma^{a}}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{V}^{\Gamma^{b}}({\bf m})}{f_{\bf b}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C|\mathbf{m}|)^{\Gamma^{b}}\!\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{l_{i,j}}}{l_{i,j}!}\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{l}|^{2}}{n\delta^{2}}\right)^{\Gamma^{a}}

(107)

\displaystyle=(C|\mathbf{m}|)^{|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{l_{i,j}}}{l_{i,j}!}\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{l}|^{2}|\mathbf{m}|}{n\delta^{2}}\right)^{\Gamma^{a}}.

Since
\Gamma^{b}
is determined by
\Gamma^{a}
it is sufficient to sum over all possible
\Gamma^{a}
. If
n^{1-3\eta}>2C/\delta^{2}
, the dominating term in the sum of bounds is that corresponding to the smallest possible
\Gamma^{a}
. We aim to give a give a lower bound to
\Gamma^{a}
under the constraint that the inner product is non-zero.

As noted before the necessary condition for the inner product to be non-zero is
m_{S}^{\bf a}=m_{S}^{\bf b}
. We consider a lower bound for
\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})+\Gamma({f_{\bf b}})
which will in turn give a lower bound for
\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})
. We start with two tableaux
t_{\bf a^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
and
t_{\bf b^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf l})
so that
\Gamma(f_{{\bf a}^{\prime}})=\Gamma(f_{{\bf a}^{\prime}})=0
. Since
|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta}
and
\lambda_{r}\geq\delta n+O(n^{\alpha})
, the tableau
t_{\bf a^{\prime}}
can be chosen to have at most one modified box per column so the last condition is met (and similarly for
t_{\bf b^{\prime}}
). We also ensure that each of the modified columns of
t_{\bf a}
(or
t_{\bf b}
) are also modified in
t_{\bf a^{\prime}}
(or
t_{\bf b^{\prime}}
). The idea is to count the minimum number of ‘horizontal box shuffling’ operations necessary
in order to transform the Young tableau
t_{\bf a^{\prime}}
into the tableau
t_{\bf a}
and
t_{\bf b^{\prime}}
into the given tableau
t_{\bf b}
so that at the end
m_{S}^{\bf a}=m_{S}^{\bf b}
.

The horizontal box shuffling is described as follows; at each step we horizontally move one elementary column modifier
\kappa(i,j)
of
t_{{\bf a}^{\prime}}
(or
t_{{\bf b}^{\prime}}
) into an already modified
column, with the aim of constructing
t_{\bf a}
(or
t_{\bf b}
). Note that such operation increases
\Gamma(f_{{\bf a}^{\prime}})+\Gamma(f_{{\bf b}^{\prime}})
by one and also changes

m_{S}^{{\bf a}^{\prime}}
(or
m_{S}^{{\bf b}^{\prime}}
) in the following way. The multiplicities

m_{\{(i,-),(j,+)\}}
and
m_{S_{0}}
decrease by one, and
m_{S_{0}+\{(i,-),(j,+)\}}
increases by one. Here
S_{0}
is the set of modified entries for the column to which the box
(i,j)
is moved. Hence the distance

\sum_{S}|m_{S}^{{\bf a}^{\prime}}-m_{S}^{{\bf b}^{\prime}}|

decreases by at most three. Since initially this quantity was equal to
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}|l_{i,j}-m_{i,j}|
(because all the column modifiers were elementary in
t_{{\bf a}^{\prime}}
and
t_{{\bf b}^{\prime}}
), we need at least
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}|{l}_{i,j}-{m}_{i,j}|/3
such operations before reaching our goal

m_{S}^{\bf a}=m_{S}^{\bf b}
. Hence we have
\Gamma({f_{\bf a}})+\Gamma({f_{\bf b}})\geq|\mathbf{l}-\mathbf{m}|/3
.

Finally using
\Gamma^{b}-\Gamma^{a}=|{\bf m}|-|{\bf l}|
, we obtain
\Gamma^{a}\geq(|\mathbf{l}-\mathbf{m}|+3|\mathbf{l}|-3|\mathbf{m}|)/6
and hence the desired result ((
99
)).

Proof of (
101
).

Since
\mathbf{l}=\mathbf{m}
, equations (
90
) and (
95
) prove that the bound (
107
) is saturated when
\Gamma^{a}=0
, up to the error factor
\left(1+O(n^{-1+2\eta}/\delta)\right)
. Hence the remainder
term due to the other
\Gamma
consist in a geometric series with factor

\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{m}|^{3}}{n\delta^{2}}\right)=O(n^{1-3\eta}/\delta^{2})
.

∎

D.2
Non-orthogonality issues and construction of
V^{r}_{\lambda}

In this subsection we consider the basis vectors
|\bf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle
and show that they are approximately orthogonal. We will use this result to construct the isometry
V^{r}_{\lambda}
and hence the channel
T^{r}_{\lambda}
.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}
and
\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}
be two basis vectors (corresponding to two semistandard Young tableaux with diagram
\lambda
) and let
|{\bf m}|:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}m_{ij}
and

|{\bf l}-{\bf m}|:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}|l_{i,j}-m_{ij}|
.

If

\sum_{j>i}m_{i,j}-\sum_{j<i}m_{j,i}\neq\sum_{j>i}l_{i,j}-\sum_{j<i}l_{j,i}

for some
1\leq i\leq r
, then

\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle=0.

Otherwise, we derive an upper bound under the following conditions.
We assume that
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i+1}>\delta n
for all
1\leq i\leq r-1
and
\lambda_{r}>\delta n
, for some
\delta>0
. Furthermore we assume

|\mathbf{l}|\leq|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}
for some
\eta<1/3
and that

Cn^{3\eta-1}/\delta^{2}<1
where
C=C(d)
is a constant.

Then:

\left|\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|\leq(C^{\prime}n)^{-\eta(|{\bf m}|-|{\bf l}|)/4}\,(C^{\prime}n)^{(9\eta-2)|{\bf m}-{\bf l}|/12}\,\delta^{(|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|)/2-|\mathbf{m}-\mathbf{l}|/3}\,(1+O(n^{-1+3\eta}/\delta)).

(108)

where
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(d,\eta)
and the constant in the remainder term depends only on
d
. The right side is of order less than
n^{(9\eta-2)|{\bf m}-{\bf l}|/12}
and converges to zero for
\eta<2/9
when
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

We know that
\left|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right\rangle
is a linear combination
of
n
-tensor product vectors in which the basis vector
f_{i}
appears exactly

\lambda_{i}-\sum_{j>i}m_{i,j}+\sum_{j<i}m_{j,i}
times for
1\leq i\leq r
. As two tensor basis vectors are orthogonal if they do not have the same number of
f_{i}
in the decomposition, we get that
\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle=0
if
\sum_{j>i}m_{i,j}-\sum_{j<i}m_{j,i}\neq\sum_{j>i}l_{i,j}-\sum_{j<i}l_{j,i}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.

Now consider the following expansion of the inner product,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}\rangle\langle q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}\rangle}}

(109)

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}\rangle\langle p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf l}}\rangle}}

(110)

where we have used the fact that
q_{\lambda}
is a projection (use (
39
) and (
40
) except that we have only
r
rows in our Young diagrams), up to a constant factor. Now we decompose
p_{\lambda}f_{\bf m}
and
p_{\lambda}f_{\bf l}

on orbits as in (
84
).
Since the multiplicity of the elements in the orbits are the same in numerator and denominator, we obtain:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle=\frac{\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf b}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf a^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a^{\prime}}}\rangle\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf b}}|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf b^{\prime}}}\rangle}}.

(111)

We use (
101
) for the denominator:

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf a^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{m})}{f_{\bf a^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\Bigg\langle\sum_{{f_{\bf b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf b}}\Bigg|q_{\lambda}\sum_{{f_{\bf b^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{l})}{f_{\bf b^{\prime}}}\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{(m_{i,j}+l_{i,j})/2}}{\sqrt{m_{i,j}!\,l_{i,j}!}}(1+O(n^{3\eta-1}/\delta)),

and the numerator is bounded as in (
99
). Then, under the assumption

|{\bf m}|\geq|{\bf l}|
we have

\left|\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|\leq(C|\mathbf{m}|)^{|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|}\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{m}|^{3}}{\delta^{2}n}\right)^{\Gamma_{min}}\cdot\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}\sqrt{\frac{m_{i,j}!}{l_{i,j}!}}\cdot\left(1+O(n^{3\eta-1}/\delta)\right),

where
\Gamma_{min}=((|\mathbf{l}-\mathbf{m}|+3|\mathbf{l}|-3|\mathbf{m}|)/6)\wedge 0
.

The factorials can be bounded as

\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\sqrt{\frac{m_{i,j}!}{l_{i,j}!}}\leq|\mathbf{m}|^{\sum(m_{i,j}-l_{i,j})_{+}/2}=|\mathbf{m}|^{(|\mathbf{m}-\mathbf{l}|+|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|)/4}.

Since
|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta}
and
Cn^{3\eta-1}/\delta^{2}<1
, we have

\left(\frac{C|\mathbf{m}|^{3}}{\delta^{2}n}\right)^{\Gamma_{min}}\leq\left(\frac{Cn^{3\eta-1}}{\delta^{2}}\right)^{(|\mathbf{l}-\mathbf{m}|+3|\mathbf{l}|-3|\mathbf{m}|)/6}.

Since
n\delta<\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}<n
we have

(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}\leq n^{{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}}\quad\text{ if }l_{i,j}\geq m_{i,j}

and

(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}\leq(n\delta)^{{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}}\quad\text{ if }l_{i,j}<m_{i,j}

so that we have

\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{(l_{i,j}-m_{i,j})/2}\leq n^{(|{\bf l}|-|{\bf m}|)/2}\delta^{\sum(m_{i,j}-l_{i,j})_{+}/2}\leq(n\delta)^{(|{\bf l}|-|{\bf m}|)/2}.

The last inequality follows since
\delta<1
and by assumption
|{\bf l}|-|{\bf m}|\leq 0
.

The constant
C=C(d)
can be replaced by another constant

C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(d,\eta)
such that all powers of
n
appear in the form
(C^{\prime}n)^{\gamma}
. The above bound yields

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|\leq\delta^{(|\mathbf{m}|-|\mathbf{l}|)/2-|\mathbf{m}-\mathbf{l}|/3}(C^{\prime}n)^{-\eta(|{\bf m}|-|{\bf l}|)/4}(C^{\prime}n)^{(9\eta-2)|{\bf m}-{\bf l}|/12}(1+O(n^{-1+3\eta}/\delta))

∎

We have the following lemma which shows that for a given
\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}
, its overlap with
\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}
s is uniformly small.

Lemma D.4
.

Let
\eta<2/9
and let
\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}
be such that
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}
. Assume as in Lemma
D.3
that
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i+1}>\delta n
for all
1\leq i\leq r-1
and
\lambda_{r}>\delta n
, for some
\delta>0
, and that

Cn^{3\eta-1}/\delta^{2}<1
where
C=C(d)
is a constant.

Then there exists a constant
C^{\prime\prime}=C^{\prime\prime}(d,\eta)
such that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{l}|\leq n^{\eta}\\
\mathbf{l}\neq\mathbf{m}\end{subarray}}\left|\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|\leq(C^{\prime\prime}n)^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3}.

(112)

Proof.

We break the sum into two parts (
|{\bf l}|\leq|{\bf m}|
and
|{\bf l}|>|{\bf m}|
), and we note it suffices to prove the statement under the additional condition

|{\bf l}|\leq|{\bf m}|
(otherwise we use (
108
) with the roles of
\bf{l}
and
\bf{m}
exchanged).

We use (
108
) neglecting the terms containing
|{\bf m}|-|{\bf l}|
in the exponent which are less than
1
. Then the expression on the left side of
(
112
) is bounded from above by

2\sum_{k\geq 1}N(k)\left[(C^{\prime}n)^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3}\right]^{k}

where
N(k)
is the number of
{\bf l}
’s for which
|{\bf m}-{\bf l}|=k
.

Since there are at most
rd
pairs
1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d
, there are at most

(k+1)^{rd}
different choices for the values
\{|l_{i,j}-m_{i,j}|:1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d\}
satisfying

\sum|l_{i,j}-m_{i,j}|=k
. Moreover, there are
2^{rd}
sign choices which fix
{\bf l}=\{l_{i,j}\}
completely. Thus
N(k)\leq(2(k+1))^{rd}\leq c^{k}
for some constant
c
. Thus we have a geometric series with the first term corresponding to the RHS of (
112
) and hence the desired result is proved.

∎

We use this quasi-orthogonality to build an isometry

V^{r}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}^{r}
which maps the relevant
finite-dimensional vectors
\left|{\bf m}_{\lambda}\right\rangle
‘close’ to their Fock counterparts
\left|{\bf m}\right\rangle
, with
|\mathbf{m}\rangle\in\mathcal{F}^{r}
. This is the aim of following two lemmas.

Lemma D.5
.

Let
A
be a contraction (i.e.
A^{*}A\leq\bf{1}
) from a finite space
\mathcal{H}
to an infinite space
\mathcal{K}
. Then there is an
R:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{K}
such that
A+R
is an isometry and
{\rm Range}(A)\perp{\rm Range}(R)
.

As a consequence, for any unit vector
\phi
, we have
\|R\phi\|^{2}=1-\|A\phi\|^{2}
.

Proof.

See
[
36
]
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
\eta<2/9
. Suppose that
\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i+1}\geq\delta n
for all

1\leq i\leq r
, with the convention
\lambda_{r+1}=0
. Then for

n>n_{0}(\eta,\delta,d)
there exists an isometry
V^{r}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}^{r}
such that,

V^{r}_{\lambda}|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle=|{\bf 0}\rangle
and for
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}
,

\langle\mathbf{m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(\tilde{C}n)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}}\left\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right|

where
\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}(\eta,d)
is a particular constant. More precisely,

n_{0}
can be taken of the form
\left(\frac{C(d)}{\delta^{2}}\right)^{1/(1-3\eta)}
.

Proof.

Let
A^{r}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}^{r}
be defined by

A^{r}_{\lambda}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}}\sum_{|\mathbf{l}|\leq n^{\eta}}|\mathbf{l}\rangle\left\langle\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\right|.

where
|\mathbf{l}\rangle\in\mathcal{F}^{r}
.
Then,

\displaystyle A_{\lambda}^{r*}A^{r}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}\sum_{|\mathbf{l}|\leq n^{\eta}}\left|\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\right\rangle\left\langle\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}}.

where the last inequality follows from the following argument.
It is enough to show that all eigenvalues of
A_{\lambda}^{r*}A^{r}_{\lambda}
are smaller than
1
. Let
\sum_{\bf m}c_{\bf m}\left|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right\rangle
be an eigenvector of
A_{\lambda}^{r*}A^{r}_{\lambda}
, and
a
the corresponding eigenvalue. Then by the linear independence of
\left|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right\rangle
we get that for each
{\bf l}

\frac{1}{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}\sum_{|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}}\left\langle\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right\rangle c_{\bf m}=ac_{\bf l}.

If
{\bf l}_{0}
is an index for which
|c_{\bf l}|
is maximum, then by taking absolute values on both sides and dividing by
|c_{\bf l_{0}}|
, we obtain

a\leq\frac{1}{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}\sum_{|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}}\left|\langle\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|\leq 1

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
D.4
.
Now may apply Lemma
D.5
, and find an
R^{r}_{\lambda}
such that

A^{r}_{\lambda}+R^{r}_{\lambda}
is an isometry, and
{\rm Range}(R^{r}_{\lambda})\perp{\rm Range}(A^{r}_{\lambda})
, so that
\langle\mathbf{m}|R^{r}_{\lambda}=0
.
Defining
V^{r}_{\lambda}:=A^{r}_{\lambda}+R^{r}_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{m}|(A^{r}_{\lambda}+R^{r}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{m}|A^{r}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}}\langle\mathbf{m}|\sum_{|\mathbf{l}|\leq n^{\eta}}|\mathbf{l}\rangle\left\langle\mathbf{l}_{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(Cn)^{(9\eta-2)/12}/\delta^{1/3}}}\left\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\right|.

∎

D.3
Proof of the main lemmas

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

To obtain upper bounds for the following expression

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}}b_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}-\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(u,V_{\mu})||_{1}

one first approximates
p_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}
by a
r
dimensional multinomial distribution and then uses the classical channel
\tau_{\lambda}^{r,n}
to map the multinomial distribution to a distribution in
\mathbb{R}^{r-1}
which lies close to the multivariate normal. Thus we obtain the following variant of equation (7.35) of
[
36
]

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(u,V_{\mu})-\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}}b_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|p^{u,r,n}-M^{n}_{\mu^{u,n}_{1},\dots,\mu^{u,n}_{r}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(u,V_{\mu})-\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}}M^{n}_{\mu^{u,n}_{1},\dots,\mu^{u,n}_{r}}(\lambda)\tau_{\lambda}^{r,n}\right\|_{1},

(113)

where
M^{n}_{\mu^{u,n}_{1},\dots,\mu^{u,n}_{r}}
is the
r
-multinomial with coefficients
\mu_{i}^{u,n}
. One then shows that both the terms in the RHS is
O(n^{-1/2+\gamma}+n^{-1/4+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
and finally obtain:

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}||\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}}b_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}-\mathcal{N}_{r-1}(u,V_{\mu})||_{1}\leq C_{r}(n^{-1/2+\gamma}+n^{-1/4+\epsilon})

where
C_{r}
is a constant which depends only on the rank
r
. Since the proof is given in
[
36
]
we omit the details.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

Note that

p_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\times\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}).

where (see
[
19
]
)

\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda})=\left(\begin{subarray}{c}n\\
\\
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{r}\end{subarray}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{l}!\prod_{k=l+1}^{r}\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}+k-l}{(\lambda_{l}+r-l)!}.

The rest of the proof proceeds as in
[
36
]
. One checks that

\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\lambda_{l}!\prod_{k=l+1}^{r}\lambda_{l}-\lambda_{k}+k-l}{(\lambda_{l}+r-l)!}\leq 1

and

\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\leq 1,\quad\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\leq n^{r^{2}},

so that

\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}^{c}}p_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}\leq n^{r^{2}}\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}^{c}}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}n\\
\\
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{r}\end{subarray}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}.

Also note that

\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}^{c}=\{\lambda:\lambda_{k}=0\text{ for }k>r\text{ and }\exists i\text{ such that }|\lambda_{i}-n\mu_{i}|>n^{\alpha}\}

Thus we are summing a multinomial probability distribution over extreme values. Since each coordinate of the multinomial is a binomial random variable one uses Hoeffding inequality for Bernoulli random variables and union bound to obtain

\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}^{c}}p_{\lambda}^{u,r,n}\leq 2rn^{r^{2}}\exp(-n^{2\alpha-1}/2)

for large enough
n
.
∎

Proof of lemma
C.3
.

We start by considering the states

\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}}|\bar{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\bar{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}|

\phi^{0,r}=\sum_{\mathbf{m}_{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{r(r-1)/2}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\frac{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}|\mathbf{m}_{1}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{1}|\otimes|\mathbf{0}_{2}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{2}|.

(114)

Note that although
|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle
are eigenvectors of
\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}
, they are not orthogonal and hence the decomposition is not straightforward.
Let

\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda}=\text{ span }\{|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle:\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\lambda}\}

(115)

We split
\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda}
into direct sum of two spaces i.e.

\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\bigoplus\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}

where

\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}=\text{ span }\{|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda}:|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}\}.

Also decompose
\mathcal{F}^{r}=\mathcal{F}^{r}_{\eta}\bigoplus\mathcal{F}^{r\perp}_{\eta}
with
\{|\mathbf{m}_{1}\rangle\otimes|\mathbf{0}_{2}\rangle:|\mathbf{m}_{1}|\leq n^{\eta}\}
spanning the set
\mathcal{F}^{r}_{\eta}
. Define the projectors into the subspaces
\mathcal{H}^{r}_{\lambda,\eta},\mathcal{F}^{r}_{\eta},
and
\mathcal{F}^{r\perp}_{\eta}

as
P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta},P^{r}_{\eta},
and
P^{r\perp}_{\eta}
respectively. One can show that the following decomposition holds:

\displaystyle||T^{r}_{\lambda}(\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda})-\phi^{0,r}||_{1}\leq 2||T^{r}_{\lambda}(P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta})-P^{r}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r}_{\eta}||_{1}+2||P^{r\perp}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r\perp}_{\eta}||_{1}.

(116)

Indeed consider Hermitian trace class operators
A,B,A_{1},B_{1},A_{2},B_{2}
such that

A=A_{1}+A_{2},\quad B=B_{1}+B_{2}.

Further assume that
A,A_{1},B_{1},B_{2}
are positive semidefinite operators and
\mathrm{Tr}{A}=\mathrm{Tr}{B}=1
. Now for a Hermitian operator trace class operator
X
define

|X|=\sqrt{X^{*}X},\quad X_{+}=(|X|+X)/2,\quad X_{-}=(|X|-X)/2

so that
X=X_{+}-X_{-}
,
|X|=X_{+}+X_{-}
and
||X||_{1}=\mathrm{Tr}|X|
.

Using this decomposition into positive and negative parts we have

\displaystyle A-B

\displaystyle=A_{1}-B_{1}+A_{2}-B_{2}

\displaystyle=(A_{1}-B_{1})_{+}-(A_{1}-B_{1})_{-}+(A_{2}-B_{2})_{+}-(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}.

Since
\mathrm{Tr}(A-B)=0
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{+}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}+\mathrm{Tr}(A_{1}-B_{1})_{-}-\mathrm{Tr}(A_{1}-B_{1})_{+}

(117)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}+||A_{1}-B_{1}||_{1}.

We obtain the following upper bound

\displaystyle||A-B||_{1}

\displaystyle\leq||A_{1}-B_{1}||_{1}+||A_{2}-B_{2}||_{1}

(118)

\displaystyle=||A_{1}-B_{1}||_{1}+\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{+}+\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}

\displaystyle\leq 2||A_{1}-B_{1}||_{1}+2\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}

\displaystyle\leq 2||A_{1}-B_{1}||_{1}+2||B_{2}||_{1}.

For the second inequality we have used the relation (
117
) while for the
last inequality we have used the relation

\mathrm{Tr}(A_{2}-B_{2})_{-}=\mathrm{Tr}(B_{2}-A_{2})_{+}\leq\mathrm{Tr}(B_{2})_{+}\leq||B_{2}||_{1}.

Let
A=T^{r}_{\lambda}(\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda})
,
B=\phi^{0,r}
,
A_{1}=T^{r}_{\lambda}(P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta})
,
B_{1}=P^{r}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r}_{\eta}
, and
B_{2}=P^{r\perp}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r\perp}_{\eta}
. Then we use (
118
) to obtain (
116
).

Being a geometric sum the second term of relation (
116
) can be shown to be
\max_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(\mu_{i}/\mu_{j})^{n^{\eta}}

=O(\exp(-c_{1}n^{\eta}))
for some constant
c_{1}
. To deal with the first term note that
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}
and we have by Lemma
D.6

P(\{m_{i}\})=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}}|\bar{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\bar{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}|=\frac{1}{1+Cn^{(9\eta-2)/12}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}}|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|+E(\{m_{i}\})

and the positive remainder
E(\{m_{i}\})
has trace norm

\mathrm{Tr}(E(\{m_{i}\})=O(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}).\text{dim}(\mathcal{H}(\{m_{i}\}))

where
\mathcal{H}(\{m_{i}\})
is the space on which
P(\{m_{i}\})
projects.
Multiplying by the operator
P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}
on both sides we obtain

P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}=\frac{1}{1+Cn^{(9\eta-2)/12}}\breve{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}E(\{m_{i}\}).

(119)

where the approximate state
\breve{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}
is given by

\breve{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}\\
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}\end{subarray}}|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|.

Trace norm of the second term in (
119
) is given by

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}E(\{m_{i}\})\right)=O(n^{9\eta-2/12})

and since
T^{r}_{\lambda}
is contractive we have

||T^{r}_{\lambda}(P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}-\tilde{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda})||_{1}\leq||P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}\rho^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}P^{r}_{\lambda,\eta}-\tilde{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda}||_{1}=O(n^{9\eta-2/12}).

Thus we need to give an upper bound to
||T^{r}_{\lambda}(\breve{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda})-P^{r}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r}_{\eta}||_{1}
in order to give an upper bound to the RHS of (
116
).
Since
\mu_{i}^{u,n}=\mu_{i}+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma})
for
|m|\leq n^{\eta}
we have

\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}=\left(\frac{\mu_{i}}{\mu_{j}}\right)^{m_{ij}}(1+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta}))

and hence the from the definition of
N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}
we have

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\right]^{-1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda},|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda},|\mathbf{m}|>n^{\eta}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}(1+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta}))

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda},|\mathbf{m}|>n^{\eta}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}(1+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta}))

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda},|\mathbf{m}|>n^{\eta}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}.

Again by a geometric sum argument the last two terms can easily be seen to be of the order

O(\exp(-c_{2}n^{\eta}))
for some constant
c_{2}
and hence we obtain

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\right)^{m_{ij}}(1+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta}))

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\frac{\mu_{i}}{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}(1+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta}))

(120)

By definition of
\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda}

m_{i,j}=0
unless
i,j\leq r
. Using the fact that
\||\psi\rangle\langle\psi|-|\psi^{\prime}\rangle\langle\psi^{\prime}|\|_{1}=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi|\psi^{\prime}\rangle|^{2}}
(see (2)) holds for unit vectors (
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
) in conjunction with Lemma
D.6
we obtain

\displaystyle||T^{r}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{\mathbf{m}}_{\lambda}|)-|\mathbf{m}_{1}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{1}|\otimes|\mathbf{0}_{2}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{2}|||_{1}

\displaystyle=||V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|V^{r*}_{\lambda}-|\mathbf{m}_{1}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{1}|\otimes|\mathbf{0}_{2}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{2}|)||_{1}

\displaystyle=O(n^{9\eta-2/24}).

Note that we have ignored the
\delta
since the minimum gap between the eigenvalues is fixed. Consequently

\displaystyle T^{r}_{\lambda}(\tilde{\rho}^{0,u,r,n}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}\\
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}\end{subarray}}T^{r}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{u,r,n}_{\lambda}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}(\mu^{u,n}_{i})^{\lambda_{i}}\sum_{\{m_{i}\}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\left(\frac{\mu^{u,n}_{j}}{\mu^{u,n}_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{r}\cap\{m_{i}\}\\
|\mathbf{m}|\leq n^{\eta}\end{subarray}}T^{r}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{\lambda}|)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\mathbf{m}_{1}|\leq n^{\eta}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\frac{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\mu_{i}}\right)^{m_{i,j}}|\mathbf{m}_{1}\rangle\langle\mathbf{m}_{1}|\otimes|\mathbf{0}_{2}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{2}|+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta},n^{9\eta-2/24})

\displaystyle=P^{r}_{\eta}\phi^{0,r}P^{r}_{\eta}+O(n^{-1/2+\gamma+\eta},n^{9\eta-2/24})

which was to be shown.

∎

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Similar to the lemmas in subsections D.1 and D.2 this lemma will be presented from the original Lemma 6.4 of
[
36
]

mutatis mutandis
for the convenience of the reader. Recall that
|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|
is the vacuum in the multimode Fock space
\mathcal{F}^{r}
where the number of modes is
r(r-1)/2+r(d-r)
. Let us define

D^{\mathfrak{z}_{1}+\mathbf{z},\mathfrak{z}_{2}}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)=|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|.

Although
\mathfrak{z}
and
\mathbf{z}
are of different dimension we can identify
\mathbf{z}
as
(\mathbf{z},0)
, so that

|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle
makes sense. Also note that
|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|
is a pure state in the multimode Fock space
\mathcal{F}^{r}
.

Similarly define
|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle=U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}\rangle
and hence

T^{r}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},r,n}T^{r*}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)=V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda|V^{r*}_{\lambda}.

According to Lemma
D.6
, the coordinates of

V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle

in the Fock basis are described by:

\displaystyle\langle{\bf m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\langle{\bf m}_{\lambda}|U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle(1+O(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3}))\mbox{ if }|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta};\\
\mbox{something not important if }|{\bf m}|>n^{\eta}.\end{array}\right.

To show that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta,||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}||(T^{r}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},r,n}T^{r*}_{\lambda}-D^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}})(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)||_{1}\rightarrow 0

and

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta,||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}||(D^{\mathbf{z},0}-T^{r}_{\lambda}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathbf{z},r,n}T^{r*}_{\lambda})(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)||_{1}\rightarrow 0

it is enough to show that the first limit goes to
0
since the second one is a special case of the first. Recalling
(2) we observe that it is enough to show

\sup_{||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\mathfrak{z}\in\Theta}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}1-|\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle|=R(n)^{2}

since
V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda|V^{*}_{\lambda}
and
|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|
are pure states. Here
R(n)
goes to
0
(we will give an explicit expression of this term later).

Recall that
\mathbf{m}=\{m_{i,j}\}_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}
and also note that we need the full
\mathbf{m}
here in sharp contrast with
\bf{m}_{1}
(i.e.
\bf{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\lambda}
) used in Lemma
C.3
. First we decompose the inner product as follows

\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle=\sum_{m}\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|\bf{m}\rangle\langle\bf{m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},\lambda\rangle

(123)

Consider the following set

\mathcal{M}:=\{{\bf m}:m_{i,j}\leq|(\mathfrak{z}+z)_{i,j}|^{2}n^{\epsilon},\quad 1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d\}.

(124)

In particular, since
2\beta+\epsilon<\eta
we have
\mathcal{M}\subset\{{\bf m}:|{\bf m}|\leq n^{\eta}\}
.

We can upper bound the overlap of the coherent state with
|\bf{m}\rangle
s where
m\notin\mathcal{M}
as follows:

\sum_{\mathbf{m}\not\in\mathcal{M}}|\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|{\bf m}\rangle|^{2}\leq\sum_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\exp(-x_{i,j})\sum_{k>x_{i,j}n^{\epsilon}}\frac{x_{ij}^{k}}{k!}\leq rdn^{-\epsilon},\qquad\text{ where }x_{i,j}=|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|^{2}_{i,j}.

(125)

The last inequality follows since each of the terms in the sum is a tail of Poisson distribution and is bounded by

n^{-\epsilon}
.

From the first line of (
D.3
) we get

\displaystyle\langle{\bf m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle y_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|y_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|f_{\bf 0}\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle y_{\lambda}{f_{\bf 0}}|y_{\lambda}{f_{\bf 0}}\rangle}\sqrt{\langle y_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|y_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}\rangle}}(1+O(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|f_{\bf 0}\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}|q_{\lambda}p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}\rangle}}(1+O(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3})).

The first line follows since
y_{\lambda}
and
U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})
commutes and for the second line we have used (
40
), (
42
) and (
A
).

We recall that
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})
is the orbit in
(\mathbb{C}^{d})^{\otimes n}
of
{f_{\bf m}}
under
\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}
and that we have the decomposition

p_{\lambda}{f_{\bf m}}=\sum_{{f_{\bf a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}\frac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}}{\#\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}{f_{\bf a}}.

Using (
9
) and (
101
), we can write

\displaystyle\langle{\bf m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{f_{\bf{a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}\langle f_{\bf{a}}|q_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})f_{\mathbf{0}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\sum_{f_{\bf{a}},f_{\bf{b}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}({\bf m})}\langle f_{\bf{a}}|q_{\lambda}f_{\bf{b}}\rangle}}(1+O(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3})

(126)

\displaystyle=e^{-\|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\|^{2}_{2}/2}\prod_{1\leq i\leq r,i<j\leq d}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}+z)_{i,j}^{m_{i,j}}}{\sqrt{m_{i,j}!}}\left(\frac{n(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\right)^{m_{i,j}/2}r(n).

The corresponding remainder term is

r(n)=1+O\left(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3},n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon}\delta^{-3/2},n^{-1+\alpha+2\beta}\delta^{-1},\right.\\
\left.n^{-1+\alpha+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+3\eta}\delta^{-1}\right).

Since
\lambda\in\Lambda_{2}
and the eigenvalues are separated by
\delta
we have
\left(\frac{n(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\right)^{m_{i,j}/2}=1+O(n^{\alpha-1+\eta}/\delta)
and the error can be absorbed in
r(n)
.

Thus, for
\mathbf{m}
satisfying (
124
), we have:

\langle{\bf m}|V^{r}_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle=\langle{\bf m}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle r(n).

If
a_{\bf m}
and
b_{\bf m}
are the two sets of coefficients, such that

\sum_{\bf m}|a_{\bf m}|^{2}=\sum_{\bf m}|b_{\bf m}|^{2}=1
, then

1-\left|\sum_{\bf m}a_{\bf m}b_{\bf m}\right|\leq 1-\left|\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathcal{M}}a_{\bf m}b_{\bf m}\right|+\left|\sum_{{\bf m}\notin\mathcal{M}}a_{\bf m}b_{\bf m}\right|\leq 2\left(1-\left|\sum_{{\bf m}\in\mathcal{M}}a_{\bf m}b_{\bf m}\right|\right).

(127)

We use (
123
) in conjunction with
(
125
) and (
127
), we get

\displaystyle 1-\left|\langle\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}|V^{r}_{\lambda}U^{r}_{\lambda}((\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z})/\sqrt{n})|{\bf 0}_{\lambda}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(1-r(n),\sum_{\mathbf{m}\not\in\mathcal{M}}|\langle{\bf m}|\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z}\rangle|^{2}\right)=R_{2}(n),

(128)

with

R_{2}(n)=O\left(n^{(9\eta-2)/12}\delta^{-1/3},n^{-1+2\beta+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1/2+3\beta+2\epsilon}\delta^{-3/2},n^{-1+\alpha+2\beta}\delta^{-1},\right.\\
\left.n^{-1+\alpha+\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-1+3\eta}\delta^{-1},n^{-\epsilon}\right).

Note that we are interested in the square root of the LHS of (
128
). Setting
R(n)=\sqrt{R_{2}(n)}
and ignoring
\delta
in the final statement (since it is a fixed constant) we obtain the desired result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

Note that although
SU(d)
forms a group the unitaries
U^{r}
do not form a subgroup. Instead we view the unitaries as elements of
SU(d)
and the corresponding
\Delta^{\mathbf{z},r,n}_{\lambda}
as
\Delta^{\mathbf{z},n}_{\lambda}

and directly use Lemma 6.5 of
[
36
]
which states that

\sup_{||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{n,\beta,\gamma}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n,\alpha}}||(\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},n}-\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z},n}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathbf{z},n})(|\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}|)||_{1}=R(n)

Note that in our case also
T^{r*}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)=|\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}|
. Finally we identify
\Delta^{\mathbf{z},r,n}_{\lambda}
s as
\Delta^{\mathbf{z},n}_{\lambda}
s as mentioned and since we are taking supremum over restricted sets (for the coordinates of
\theta
with
i,j\geq r
is
0
and
\lambda
is further restricted to
\Lambda_{1}
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta,||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}}||(\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},r,n}-\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z},r,n}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathbf{z},r,n})T^{r*}_{\lambda}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)||_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{||\mathbf{z}||\leq n^{\beta}}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{n,\beta,\gamma}}\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{n,\alpha}}||(\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z}+\mathbf{z},n}-\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{z},n}\Delta_{\lambda}^{\mathbf{z},n})(|\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}_{\lambda}|)||_{1}=R(n).

∎

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

Define
u_{r}=\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}u_{i}
and
\hat{u}_{r}=\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\hat{u}_{i}

.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{U}^{c}}E_{u}[\mathcal{L}(u,\hat{u})]d\pi_{1}(u)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{U}^{c}}E_{u}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(u_{i}-\hat{u}_{i})^{2}+(\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(u_{i}-\hat{u}_{i}))^{2}\right]d\pi_{1}(u)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{r}\int_{\mathcal{U}^{c}}E_{u}\left[(u^{2}_{i}+\hat{u}^{2}_{i})\right]d\pi_{1}(u)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sqrt{E(u^{4}_{i})}\sqrt{\pi_{1}(\mathcal{U}^{c})}+rn^{2\gamma}\pi_{1}(\mathcal{U}^{c}).

In the first part we have used Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and in the second part we have used the fact that since
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
each component
|\hat{u}_{i}|\leq n^{\gamma}
for
i=1,\ldots,r-1
and hence
|\hat{u}_{r}|\leq rn^{\gamma}
.

Note that for
i=1,\ldots,r-1
,
u_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,n^{2\gamma_{0}})
and hence
Eu_{i}^{4}=3n^{4\gamma_{0}}
. It can be easily seen that
u_{r}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,(r-1)n^{2\gamma_{0}})
and so
Eu_{r}^{4}=3(r-1)^{2}n^{2\gamma_{0}}
. Also
P(|u_{i}|\geq n^{\gamma})\leq c\exp(-n^{2\gamma}/2n^{2\gamma_{0}})
for
i=1,\ldots,r-1
, so that by union bound we have

\pi(\mathcal{U}^{c})\leq rc\exp(-n^{2\gamma}/2n^{2\gamma_{0}})
. Thus we have

\int_{\mathcal{U}^{c}}E_{u}[\mathcal{L}(u,\hat{u})]d\pi_{1}(u)\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sqrt{3r^{2}n^{4\gamma_{0}}}\sqrt{rc\exp(-n^{2\gamma}/2n^{2\gamma_{0}}}+rn^{2\gamma}rc\exp(-n^{2\gamma}/2n^{2\gamma_{0}})

and the RHS of the above inequality tends to
0
since
\gamma_{0}<\gamma
.

For the quantum part we have

\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{c}}E_{\xi}[\mathcal{L}(\xi,\hat{\xi})]d\pi_{2}(\xi)=\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{c}}E_{\xi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{1\leq i\leq r}\\
{i<j\leq d}\end{subarray}}(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})|\xi_{ij}-\hat{\xi}_{ij}|^{2}d\pi_{2}(\xi).

It is enough to show that
\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{c}}E_{\xi}||\xi_{ij}-\hat{\xi}_{ij}||^{2}d\pi_{2}(\xi)\rightarrow 0
for each pair of
i,j
. As in the classical part we have the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{c}}E_{\xi}|\xi_{ij}-\hat{\xi}_{ij}|^{2}d\pi_{2}(\xi)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{c}}E_{\xi}\left[||\xi_{ij}||^{2}+||\hat{\xi}_{ij}||^{2}\right]d\pi_{2}(\xi)

\displaystyle\leq 2(\sqrt{E|(\xi_{ij})_{1}|^{4}}+\sqrt{E|(\xi_{ij})_{2}|^{4}})\sqrt{\pi_{2}(\mathcal{Z}^{c})}+4n^{2\beta}\pi_{2}(\mathcal{Z}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{3n^{4\beta_{0}}}\sqrt{rdc\exp(-n^{2\beta}/2n^{2\beta_{0}}}+4n^{2\beta}rdc\exp(-n^{2\beta}/2n^{2\beta_{0}}).

Again the RHS goes to
0
as
\beta_{0}<\beta
.
∎

Proof of Lemma 5.3.

Consider the first order approximation of the state

\rho_{\theta,r}
i.e. consider the expression
in (
79
). Then we have,

||\tilde{\rho}_{\theta^{(1)},r}-\tilde{\rho}_{\theta^{(2)},r}||^{2}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(u^{(1)}_{i}-u^{(2)}_{i})^{2}+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{1\leq i\leq r}\\
{i<j\leq d}\end{subarray}}|\zeta^{(1)}_{ij}-\zeta^{(2)}_{ij}|^{2}

Replacing
u_{i}
by
u_{i}/\sqrt{n}
and
\zeta_{ij}
by
\mathfrak{z}_{ij}\sqrt{\frac{(\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}{n}}
we obtain the desired result with the error term
O\left(\frac{||\theta^{(1)}||^{3},||\theta^{(2)}||^{3}}{n^{3/2}}\right)
corresponding to the error in first order approximation.
∎
